Potential algorithmic improvements:
1. Use CVXPY to solve convex relaxation for each active lag? But nonconvex; maybe turn into trust region: since h ∈[0,1], product h_i h_j is concave? Actually h_i h_j is bilinear indefinite. But on [0,1], fixing one variable, linear in other. Could use alternating minimization / ADMM maximizing product? Objective min max h_i + h_j - h_i h_j. For n=160, coordinate descent can be fast and find small improvements. For each i, changing h_i affects T_k for k around i (O(n) lags) not all; can compute delta exactly O(n). This could polish binary/continuous. Previous didn't do per-coordinate updates. We can implement a coordinate descent that for each coordinate solves optimal h_i given others to minimize max? Since objective max of linear in h_i (each T_k is linear in h_i for fixed others): T_k = const + h_i * g_k_i (where g either 1-h_j or -h_j). We want choose x∈[0,1] minimizing max_k (base_k + x*g_k). This is a 1D convex piecewise-linear problem! This is a powerful exact coordinate optimization. For fixed other coordinates, C5(h_i) = max_k (A_k + h_i B_k) * dx, with h_i∈[0,1]. We can find exactly the minimizer in [0,1] by checking breakpoints or using minimax linear. Then coordinate descent monotonically decreases max (C5) without affecting sum if we don't change sum? Wait changing h_i changes sum constraint. We need maintain sum n/2. Coordinate change alone violates sum. We can do paired coordinate updates (i,j) to keep sum, or project after each update (changing many). Pairwise update is 2D piecewise linear? In 2D, fixed others, each T_k = A_k + h_i b_ik + h_j c_kj; we need minimize max over box h_i,h_j∈[0,1], h_i+h_j=const (line segment). This is a 1D problem too! For pair with sum fixed S, set h_j=S-h_i, each T_k = A_k + h_i (b_ik - c_kj) + S c_kj, linear in h_i. Minimize max over h_i∈[max(0,S-1), min(1,S)]. Great. This allows exact coordinate descent maintaining sum exactly. This is likely much better than projected gradient and previous.

We can implement pair-updates (swap mass) and maybe multiple coordinates. For each pair (i,j) with current sum S (0≤S≤2), define x=h_i, y=S-x. For all affected terms except quadratic (those involving both p and q or self), expressions are linear: A_d + B_d * x. We can aggregate for each d: linear_sum[d] = const + coeff*x. Some d may have multiple affected terms (e.g. p first with r and q second with same r? diff p-r and q-r distinct unless p=q; rarely same d if pairs symmetric? Could same d for (p,r) and (q,r') if r'-? d different generally). But number small; we can aggregate in a dict/arrays of length L with zeros.
Then f(x) = max( base[d] + linear_sum_const[d] + x*linear_sum_coeff[d] + quad_terms(d,x) ). The quadratic terms count 4: self p, self q, cross (p,q), (q,p). We can add by evaluating explicitly after computing max over linear ~ base+lin. But if a quadratic term's d same as linear, max over d needs combine. We can create a small list of affected d for quadratics to handle: For each d, value = base[d] + lin_val[d] + sum quad_val(d,x). For other d, value = base[d] + lin_val[d]. To compute max, we can take np.max(base + lin_val) and also compute affected d values and take max with those. But base+lin_val has previous contributions for affected linear terms as well; for quad d, we need replace? We included linear terms excluding all quadratic terms, but for quad d, base already has unaffected terms. We did not include quadratic current; linear_sum only linear affected. So base[d]+lin_val[d] is correct for all except we must add quad. For max, compute arr = base + lin_val (O(L) per f? We can precompute base_plus? For candidate x, arr=base + const + coeff*x. That's O(L) vectorized. Then for each quad d, compute arr[d]+quad and replace. Then max = max(np.max(arr), computed). This is O(L) per f. L=319 for n=160, good; for 512 L=1023. 10k eval =>10M operations, good.

Precompute per pair base (length L), const (length L), coeff(length L), quad_ds list (d and type). base = I - current contributions of all affected terms (including linear and quad). const/coeff: For each linear affected term, subtract current contribution already in base; add expression in terms of x: term = a0 + a1 x. So const[d]+=a0, coeff[d]+=a1. For quadratic term, base subtract current, not in const/coeff; quad list stores d and coefficients for expression.

Then f(x):
arr = base + const + coeff*x  (numpy vector operations)
for (d, quad_coefs) in quad_list: val = arr[d] + q0 + q1*x + q2*x^2; arr[d]=val (maybe don't modify? can modify copy arr)
return np.max(arr)*dx

We need derive const/coeff for linear terms. Let's write helper build_pair_problem(n,h,I,p,q). It returns L, base, const, coeff, quad (list of (idx, c0,c1,c2)), x_low,x_high.
Algorithm:
Initialize base = I.copy(); const=zeros(L); coeff=zeros(L); quad=[]; affected_current_terms list? We'll just process all r from 0 to n-1 where r≠p,q for four linear terms, plus four special.
Linear terms:
1. a=p,b=r (first p, second r): d=p-r. current = x0*(1-h_r). expression = x*(1-h_r) = (1-h_r)*x. const +=0, coeff +=(1-h_r). base[idx] -= current.
2. a=q,b=r: d=q-r. current = y0*(1-h_r); expression=(S-x)*(1-h_r)= (1-h_r)*S - (1-h_r)*x. const += (1-h_r)*S; coeff += -(1-h_r). base -= current.
3. a=r,b=p: d=r-p. expression=h_r*(1-x)= h_r - h_r*x. const +=h_r; coeff += -h_r; base -= h_r*(1-x0).
4. a=r,b=q: d=r-q. expression=h_r*(1-y)=h_r*(1 - S + x). const += h_r*(1-S); coeff += h_r. base -= h_r*(1-y0).
Special:
5. self p: d=0. current=x0(1-x0); expression=x-x² = 0 +1*x -1*x². base[0_idx] -= current; quad append (0_idx,0,1,-1) maybe combine if multiple? p and q both d=0; cross d=p-q and q-p, no other d=0. We can have two quad entries same d; f will add both. Or combine.
6. self q: d=0 expression=(S-x)(1-(S-x)) =? Expand: y-y² = (S-x) - (S-x)² = S - x - (S² -2Sx +x²)= S -S² + (2S-1)x - x². So q0=S-S², q1=2S-1, q2=-1. base[0]-=current y0(1-y0). append.
7. cross a=p,b=q: d=p-q. expression=x(1-y)=x(S-x) = S x - x²: q0=0,q1=S,q2=-1. base -= x0(1-y0).
8. cross a=q,b=p: d=q-p. expression=y(1-x)=(S-x)(1-x)= S - Sx -x + x² = S + (-(S+1)) x +1*x². Wait (S-x)(1-x)=S - Sx - x + x² = S - (S+1)x + x². q0=S, q1=-(S+1), q2=1. base -= y0(1-x0).
Note: These special expressions valid even if p/q; yes.
Potential issue: For linear terms, if r equals p or q we skipped; good. If p and q adjacent? No overlap.
But if p or q? base initial I contains all terms. We subtract current for each affected term exactly once. Need ensure ordered pair (r,p) and (p,r) are different; both processed for r. Good. Self loops not in distinct but I0 contains each self term; subtract both. Cross terms both processed. For r=n? all others.

Can we vectorize update_I_from_pair? For each r, indices differ; loop. Could use h arrays and vectorized operations:
For all r except p,q, term1 contributions to idx = p - r + offset (a range decreasing). d=p-r for r≠p. This is a contiguous range of indices from p-(p-1)=1 to p-(p+1)=-1 plus p-q? Actually excluding p and q. We can update slices! Because for fixed p, r varies; d=p-r. For r=0..p-1: d positive  p..1. For r=p+1..n-1: d negative -1.. p-n+1. We can update I at these indices with vector arithmetic: coeff (1-h_r) * delta. Similarly q. This would be faster and cleaner. Let's implement slice updates.

Term1 p first: for r from 0 to n-1, r≠p,q, d=p-r. The diff d ranges over all except 0 and p-q. We can create vector diff1 = p - np.arange(n); mask r not p,q. idx1=diff1+offset. I[idx1] += delta_p * (1-h) with h array at r. delta_p = x_new - x0. Since old term x0(1-h), new x_new(1-h); diff = (x_new-x0)*(1-h_r). Yes simple. Exclude p (d=0 self) and q (cross) by mask. So vector: idx = p - np.arange(n) + offset; mask = (idx>0)&(idx<L)&(r!=p)&(r!=q)? diff excludes invalid? For r all 0..n-1, d=p-r always in [-(n-1),n-1], valid. mask=(r!=p)&(r!=q). I[idx[mask]] += delta_p*(1-h[mask]). Good.
Term2 q second? q first: d=q-r; I += (y_new-y0)*(1-h_r); y= S-x; y_new-y0 = -delta_p. So delta_y = -delta_p. I += delta_y*(1-h_r) for r≠p,q.
Term3 r first, p second: d=r-p. Term = h_r*(1-p_second). Diff = h_r*((1-x_new)-(1-x0)) = h_r*(x0-x_new) = -delta_p*h_r. So I += -delta_p*h_r at idx r-p+offset, mask r≠p,q.
Term4 r first, q second: diff = h_r*((1-y_new)-(1-y0)) = h_r*(y0-y_new)= -delta_y*h_r = delta_p*h_r. I += delta_p*h_r at idx r-q+offset, mask.
Specials: self p and q at idx0; cross p,q and q,p.
This vectorized O(n) with masks, faster. Need be careful if p<q? Not necessary.

Build pair details for line search can also be vectorized: base = I.copy(); const/coeff arrays initialized zeros. For linear terms:
Term1: d=p-r, coeff += (1-h_r); const +=0. So coeff[base_indices] += 1-h. Term2: d=q-r, coeff += -(1-h), const += S*(1-h). Term3: d=r-p, coeff += -h, const += h. Term4: d=r-q, coeff += h, const += h*(1-S). Then subtract current contributions from base:
Term1: base -= x0*(1-h); Term2: base -= y0*(1-h); Term3: base -= h*(1-x0); Term4: base -= h*(1-y0). Specials as before. This can be done vectorized with arrays r=np.arange(n), masks. Very efficient! Great.

Let's define build_pair(n,h,I,p,q):
L=2n-1; off=n-1; r=np.arange(n); hr=h[r]; mask=(r!=p)&(r!=q)
base=I.copy(); const=np.zeros(L); coeff=np.zeros(L)
idx = p - r + off; base[idx[mask]] -= x0*(1-hr[mask]); coeff[idx[mask]] += (1-hr[mask])
idx = q - r + off; base[idx[mask]] -= y0*(1-hr[mask]); const[idx[mask]] += S*(1-hr[mask]); coeff[idx[mask]] += -(1-hr[mask])
idx = r - p + off; base[idx[mask]] -= hr[mask]*(1-x0); const[idx[mask]] += hr[mask]; coeff[idx[mask]] += -hr[mask]
idx = r - q + off; base[idx[mask]] -= hr[mask]*(1-y0); const[idx[mask]] += hr[mask]*(1-S); coeff[idx[mask]] += hr[mask]
idx0=off

def compute_I(h):
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    return corr[::-1].copy()

```
def compute_I(h):
 n=len(h); I=np.zeros(2*n-1); off=n-1
 I[off]=np.dot(h,1-h)
 for d in range(1,n):
   I[off+d]=np.dot(h[d:],1-h[:-d])
   I[off-d]=np.dot(h[:-d],1-h[d:])
 return I
```
This matches our d=first-second. np.correlate max equal? It may be I reversed; but final compute_c5 uses np. However if we update I direct and optimize max(I), at end np max(I_rev) same. Good.     last_recompute = len(h)?

Candidate generation code efficient:
```
def make_candidates(h,n,I,rng,batch_min=30,batch_max=80):
    L=2*n-1; off=n-1; maxI=I.max()
    # top lags: use argpartition top 5
    top_lags = np.argpartition(I, -5)[-5:]
    top_lags = top_lags[np.argsort(I[top_lags])[::-1]]
    pairs=set()
    for idx in top_lags:
       if len(pairs)>batch_max*2: break
       d=idx-off
       if d>0:
          a=np.arange(d,n); b=np.arange(n-d)
          terms=h[a]*(1.0-h[b])
       elif d<0:
          offd=-d; a=np.arange(n-offd); b=a+offd; terms=h[a]*(1.0-h[b])
       else:
          terms=h*(1.0-h)
       if terms.size>0:
          k=min(8, terms.size)
          top_terms=np.argpartition(terms,-k)[-k:]
          if d==0:
             idxs=top_terms
             # pair indices among these
             for ii in range(idxs.size):
                for jj in range(ii+1,min(int(idxs.size),4)):
                   u=int(idxs[ii]); v=int(idxs[jj]); ...
          else:
             for t in top_terms:
                a_i=int(a[t]); b_i=int(b[t]); add sorted
       # also for each top lag, add pair of max and min? no.
    # high/low pairs
    if len(pairs)<batch_max:
       hi=np.argsort(h)[-4:]; lo=np.argsort(h)[:-4] or first 4
       for i in hi: for j in lo: add
    # random
    while len(pairs)<batch_max:
       p=rng.randint(n); q=rng.randint(n); if p!=q add
    return list(pairs)
```

Minimize scalar local:
```
def refine_pair(data, best_x, low, high, full=False):
    if full:
       res=minimize_scalar(pair_eval, bounds=(low,high), args=(data,), method='bounded', options={'xatol':1e-10,'maxiter':40})
    else:
       width=max((high-low)*0.25,1e-6)
       lo=max(low,best_x-width); hi=min(high,best_x+width)
       if hi-lo <1e-10: return best_x, pair_eval(best_x,data)
       res=minimize_scalar(... bounds=(lo,hi), maxiter=50, xatol=1e-12)
    return res.x, res.fun if finite else pair_eval
```

project_feasible:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    h=np.array(h,float).copy()
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=n*0.5
    for _ in range(100):
       diff=target-h.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
       mask=(h>1e-14)&(h<1-1e-14)
       cnt=mask.sum()
       if cnt>0: h[mask]+=diff/cnt
       else: h += diff/n
       h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    # final exact compensation by adding small residual to a free point
    diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-15:
       # find point far from bounds
       if h.size:
          idx=int(np.argmin(np.abs(h-0.5)))
          h[idx]=np.clip(h[idx]+diff,0,1)
          diff=target-h.sum()
          if abs(diff)>1e-15:
             for j in range(h.size):
                if 0<h[j]<1:
                   h[j]=np.clip(h[j]+diff,0,1); break
    return h
```
Since h may have zeros/ones; if residual cannot fit? But target within [0,n]. If all at bounds and sum off due to rounding, maybe adjust one by diff if within [0,1]. If diff not possible due to floating, skip? Should be possible. Use small eps.

compute_c5:
```
def compute_c5(h):
    n=len(h); dx=2.0/n
    corr=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr)*dx)
```
compute_I direct:
```
def compute_I(h):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; I=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    one_minus=1.0-h
    I[off]=float(np.dot(h,one_minus))
    for d in range(1,n):
        I[off+d]=float(np.dot(h[d:], one_minus[:-d]))
        I[off-d]=float(np.dot(h[:-d], one_minus[d:]))
    return I
```
For n=1? not used. For n=0? no.

interp_to:
```
def interp_to(src,src_n,dst_n):
 if src_n==dst_n return src.copy()
 xp=(np.arange(src_n)+0.5)*2.0/src_n
 xnew=(np.arange(dst_n)+0.5)*2.0/dst_n
 return np.interp(xnew,xp,src)
```
For duplicate:
```
def duplicate_to(src, dst_n):
   if dst_n % len(src)==0: return np.repeat(src, dst_n//len(src)).copy()
   return interp_to(src,len(src),dst_n)
```

Pair functions:
```
def pair_build_data(n,h,I,p,q):
    L=2*n-1; off=n-1
    x0=float(h[p]); y0=float(h[q]); S=x0+y0
    low=max(0.0,S-1.0); high=min(1.0,S)
    if high-low < 1e-13: return None
    r=np.arange(n)
    mask=(r!=p)&(r!=q)
    hr=h[r]
    base=I.copy()
    const=np.zeros(L)
    coeff=np.zeros(L)
    idx=p - r + off
    vals=1.0-hr[mask]
    base[idx[mask]] -= x0*vals
    coeff[idx[mask]] += vals
    idx=q - r + off
    base[idx[mask]] -= y0*vals? Wait vals same (1-hr), for mask same. yes.
    const[idx[mask]] += S*vals
    coeff[idx[mask]] -= vals
    idx=r - p + off
    hmask=hr[mask]
    base[idx[mask]] -= hmask*(1.0-x0)
    const[idx[mask]] += hmask
    coeff[idx[mask]] -= hmask
    idx=r - q + off
    base[idx[mask]] -= hmask*(1.0-y0)
    const[idx[mask]] += hmask*(1.0-S)
    coeff[idx[mask]] += hmask
    base[off] -= x0*(1.0-x0) + y0*(1.0-y0)
    # cross bases
    idxp=off+p-q
    idxq=off+q-p
    base[idxp] -= x0*(1.0-y0)
    base[idxq] -= y0*(1.0-x0)
    bc=base+const
    data={'low':low,'high':high,'x0':x0,'off':off,'idxp':idxp,'idxq':idxq,'S':S,
          'bc':bc,'c':coeff,'work':np.empty_like(bc)}
    return data
```
Wait in term2: base subtract y0*(1-hr), const S*vals, coeff -vals. correct.
Term4: expression h_r*(1-y) = h_r*(1-S+x). base subtract h_r*(1-y0). const h*(1-S), coeff h. correct.
Self expression sum q0=S-S^2, q1=2S, q2=-2. Cross p idx q0=0,q1=S,q2=-1; q idx q0=S,q1=-(S+1),q2=1.
Need idxp/idxq within [0,L). p-q range [-(n-1),n-1], off valid.

pair_eval:
```
def pair_eval(x, data):
    c=data['c']; work=data['work']; bc=data['bc']
    np.multiply(c, float(x), out=work)
    work += bc  # This modifies work, okay? bc+work copy? work += bc works in-place. But previously multiply filled work; then add bc. good.
    S=data['S']
    x0=x  # variable x
    # special formulas
    work[data['off']] += (S - S*S) + 2.0*S*x - 2.0*x*x
    work[data['idxp']] += S*x - x*x
    work[data['idxq']] += S - (S+1.0)*x + x*x
    return float(np.max(work))
```
Is `work += bc` okay? work overwritten each call. Yes. But we don't have separate constant; `bc=base+const`. For x, value=bc + c*x. correct.
However, using same `work` array and then `work += bc` after `multiply(c,x,out=work)` yields work = c*x + bc, good. But multiple calls: multiply overwrites work, then add bc. fine.
Need ensure special indices not overwritten by `work += bc`? They include bc; we then add specials. correct.

But note: For special self/cross, base had current contributions subtracted, and we did not include their linear parts in const; so adding specials to work after bc works. Good.

def pair_quick(data):
   low=data['low']; high=data['high']; x0=data['x0']
   length=high-low
   if length <1e-13: return None
   xs=np.array([low, high, x0, low+0.1*length, low+0.25*length, low+0.5*length, low+0.75*length, low+0.9*length, x0-0.05*length, x0+0.05*length])
   xs=np.clip(xs, low, high)
   xs=np.unique(xs)
   best=inf; bx=x0
   for xx in xs:
      v=pair_eval(float(xx), data)
      if v<best: ...
   return best,bx
```
Since pair_eval modifies work, np.unique returns new array. Good.

def pair_refine(data, quick_x, do_full=True):
    low=data['low']; high=data['high']
    candidates=[]
    candidates.append(quick_x)
    width=max((high-low)*0.25, 1e-7)
    lo=max(low, quick_x-width); hi=min(high, quick_x+width)
    if hi-lo >1e-12:
       try res=minimize_scalar(pair_eval,bounds=(lo,hi),args=(data,),method='bounded',options={'xatol':1e-11,'maxiter':50}); cand
    if do_full and high-low >1e-9:
       try res=minimize_scalar(pair_eval,bounds=(low,high),args=(data,),method='bounded',options={'xatol':1e-11,'maxiter':35}); cand
    choose min by pair_eval (res.fun may stale? use eval)
```

try_pair_update:
```
def try_pair_update(h,I,n,p,q,deadline):
    S=... if invalid return I,h,False
    data=pair_build_data(...)
    if data is None: return
    current_max=float(np.max(I))
    quick_best, quick_x = pair_quick(data)
    if quick_best >= current_max - 1e-14: return False
    # refine
    best_x,best_v = pair_refine(data, quick_x, do_full=(high-low>1e-4))
    if best_v >= current_max - 1e-14: return False
    # accept
    x_new=float(best_x); y_new=data['S']-x_new
    x0=data['x0']; y0=data['S']-x0
    delta=x_new-x0
    # update I vectorized
    r=np.arange(n); mask=(r!=p)&(r!=q); hr=h[r][mask]
    I[p - r[mask] + off] += delta*(1.0-hr)
    I[q - r[mask] + off] += -delta*(1.0-hr)
    I[r[mask] - p + off] += -delta*hr
    I[r[mask] - q + off] += delta*hr
    old_self=x0*(1-x0)+y0*(1-y0); new_self=x_new*(1-x_new)+y_new*(1-y_new)
    I[off] += new_self - old_self
    I[off+p-q] += x_new*(1-y_new) - x0*(1-y0)
    I[off+q-p] += y_new*(1-x_new) - y0*(1-x0)
    h[p]=x_new; h[q]=y_new
    return True
```
Need ensure `r[mask]` and `hr` aligned. r[mask] returns copy; okay. If n large, constructing r each try O(n). We can precompute r and masks? But pairs variable. To optimize, in try_pair_update, `r = np.arange(n)` each call costly. Could precompute base_r global? In optimize_pairs, create r_all and pass to try? We can make it parameter. But top-level function with extra arg. Let's pass r_all to try_pair_update and build_data? Build_data also constructs r. We can pass `idx_offsets`? Simpler: In optimize_pairs, `r_all=np.arange(n)` and pass to try. Pair_build_data uses r_all. Candidate gen maybe. This saves allocation.

Time/deadline inside optimize_pairs:

def optimize_pairs(h,n,deadline,seed):
   r_all=np.arange(n)
   h=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
   I=compute_I(h)
   best_h=h.copy(); best_max=float(np.max(I)); best_c5=best_max*2.0/n
   accept_count=0; total_no_improve=0
   while time.time() < deadline - 2.0:
       if accept_count % 100 ==0:
           I=compute_I(h)
       candidates=make_candidates(...)
       if not candidates: break
       improved_batch=False
       for code in candidates:
           if time.time() > deadline - 0.5: break
           p=code//n; q=code%n
           before_max=float(np.max(I))
           if try_pair_update(...):
              new_max=float(np.max(I))
              if new_max < best_max - 1e-12:
                 best_max=new_max; best_h=h.copy()
                 improved_batch=True; accept_count+=1
              else: # accepted but not best? should not happen monotone; still update? if new_max<before but >best due to drift? update if <best
                 pass
           else:
              # no immediate improvement
              pass
       if not improved_batch:
           total_no_improve +=1
           if total_no_improve > 20: break
       else:
           total_no_improve=0
   return h,best_max*2/n,n
```

def perturb_h(h,n,rng,sigma):
   hp=h.copy()
   m=min(n, max(3, n//20))
   idx=rng.choice(n,size=m,replace=False)
   hp[idx]+=rng.normal(0,sigma,m)

Now lowdim coefficient optimizer. Let's implement simple multi-start Nelder-Mead.
Helpers:
```
def fourier_y_from_theta(theta, t, modes):
    a1 = - (theta[0]/modes[0] + theta[1]/modes[1] + ...)
    y = a1*np.sin(np.pi*t)
    for a,m in zip(theta,modes): y += a*np.sin(m*np.pi*t)
    q = np.tanh(y)
    mmean = q.mean()
    y = (q - mmean) / (1.0 + abs(mmean))
    return y
```
Modes odd [3,5,7,9] (exclude 13 maybe). theta bounded? Nelder-Mead unbounded but we can bound by scaling/penalty. We can just initialize small [-1,1] and let Nelder-Mead. tanh saturates.

    return compute_c5(h)

Let's implement:
```
def fourier_y(theta,t,modes):
    y=np.zeros_like(t)
    for a,m in zip(theta,modes): y += a*np.sin(np.pi*m*t)
    q=np.tanh(y)
    mmean=float(q.mean())
    denom=1.0+abs(mmean)
    return (q-mmean)/denom
```
This is smooth except abs; use `denom=1.0+np.sqrt(mmean*mmean+1e-12)` to be smooth? Use sqrt to help minimize. Let's use `denom = 1.0 + abs(mmean)`; finite diff can handle. Or `np.hypot`. Use `math.sqrt(mmean*mmean)` no. Use `abs`.

def normalize_sum(h,n):

    mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12); cnt=...

```
_N_SL=0; _BETA_SL=1.0
def smooth_max_sl(x):
    n=_N_SL; beta=_BETA_SL; dx=2/n; corr=np.correlate(x,1-x,full); m=np.max(corr); shifted=np.minimum(beta*(corr-m),0); return (m+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(shifted)))/beta)*dx
def constraint_sl(x): return np.sum(x)-_N_SL/2
def slsqp_refine(h,n,beta,maxiter):
    global _N_SL,_BETA_SL
    _N_SL=n; _BETA_SL=beta
    bounds=[(0,1)]*n
    try:
       res=minimize(smooth_max_sl, h, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':constraint_sl}], options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'iprint':0})
       return project_feasible(res.x,n)
    except: return h.copy()
```

    if h is None or n<=0: return
    h = project_feasible(h, n)

    if np.isfinite(c): candidates.append((c,h,n))

```
def generate_fourier_candidates(deadline, seed):
    n=224; t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n; modes=[1,3,5,7,9]
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    res=[]
    starts=[]
    starts.append(np.array([0.8,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05]))
    for _ in range(3): starts.append(rng.uniform(-1,1,size=5))
    for x0 in starts:
       if time.time() > deadline - 10: break
       try:
          r=minimize(fourier_obj, x0, method='Nelder-Mead', args=(t,modes,n), options={'maxiter':80,'xatol':1e-3,'fatol':1e-7})
          theta=r.x
       except Exception:
          theta=x0
       h=0.5*(1+fourier_y(theta,t,modes))
       h=project_feasible(h,n)
       c=compute_c5(h)
       if np.isfinite(c): res.append((c,h,n))
    # Also try straightforward sign(theta) maybe? no
    return res
```
Need top-level `fourier_obj(theta,t,modes,n)`:
```
def fourier_obj(theta,t,modes,n):
    y=fourier_y(theta,t,modes)
    h=0.5*(1.0+y)
    # exact sum
    h=normalize_sum(h,n)
    return compute_c5(h)
```
`normalize_sum` should be top-level and not clip? y bounded, but after tanh absolute scaling, due to float maybe slight; clip.
```
def normalize_sum(h,n):
    h=np.array(h,float).copy()
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=0.5*n
    diff=target-np.sum(h)
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12)
       cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
       if cnt>0: h[mask]+=diff/cnt
       else: h += diff/n
       h=np.clip(h,0,1)
       diff=target-np.sum(h)
       if abs(diff)>1e-14 and n>0:
          idx=int(np.argmin(np.abs(h-0.5)))
          h[idx]=np.clip(h[idx]+diff,0,1)
    return h
```

Potential problem: Nelder-Mead with objective using `compute_c5` (exact max) may converge slowly. But 4 starts*80=320 eval; okay.

def pair_refine(data, quick_x):
    low=data['low']; high=data['high']
    best_x=quick_x; best_v=pair_eval(quick_x,data)
    length=high-low
    width=max(length*0.2,1e-8)

In `optimize_pairs`, if n<2 return initial.
```
def optimize_pairs(h,n,deadline,seed):
    if n < 2: return h, compute_c5(h), n
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    r_all=np.arange(n)
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
    I=compute_I(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_max=float(np.max(I))
    dx=2.0/n
    accept_count=0; no_improve=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 1.5:
        if accept_count % 100 ==0:
            I=compute_I(h); m=float(np.max(I))
            if m < best_max: best_max=m; best_h=h.copy()
        current_max = float(np.max(I))
        if current_max < best_max: best...
        candidates=make_candidates(h,n,I,rng)
        improved=False
        for code in candidates:
            if time.time() > deadline - 0.5: break
            p=code//n; q=code%n
            before=float(np.max(I))
            if try_pair_update(h,I,n,p,q,deadline,r_all):
                after=float(np.max(I))
                if after < before - 1e-12:
                    improved=True; accept_count+=1
                    if after < best_max:
                       best_max=after; best_h=h.copy()
                elif after < best_max and after < before:
                       best_max=after; best_h=h.copy()
        if improved:
            no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve <= 15:
                sigma = 0.02 + 0.005*no_improve
                hp = perturb_h(h,n,rng,sigma)
                Ip = compute_I(hp)
                mp = float(np.max(Ip))
                cur = float(np.max(I))
                esc = max(1.0, 0.02*max(1.0,best_max))
                if mp < best_max + esc and mp < cur + esc:
                    h=hp; I=Ip
                if mp < best_max:
                    best_max=mp; best_h=hp.copy()
            elif no_improve > 20:
                break
    # final choose
    h1=project_feasible(best_h,n); c1=compute_c5(h1)
    h2=project_feasible(h,n); c2=compute_c5(h2)
    if c2<c1: return h2,c2,n
    return h1,c1,n
```

Now, possible issue with `project_feasible`: It only adjusts sum within [0,1]. If due to floating residual slightly beyond possible, it may leave sum off by 1e-15. Acceptable? Our project_feasible final does one point then loop. Let's improve:
After loop, if diff !=0, allocate to multiple points: if diff positive, find points below 1, add min(diff, 1-h)[i] sequentially; if negative, points above 0 subtract. This ensures exact if total possible. Implement:
```
def final_fix_sum(h,n):
    target=0.5*n; diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-15: return h
    if diff>0:
       for j in np.argsort(1.0-h):
           amt=min(diff, 1.0-h[j])
           if amt>0: h[j]+=amt; diff-=amt
           if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
    else:
       for j in np.argsort(h):
           amt=min(-diff, h[j])
           if amt>0: h[j]-=amt; diff+=amt
           if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
    return h
```
This is O(n log n) only final. Use in project_feasible after loop. No closures. Let's add a helper `fix_sum_exact(h,n)`. Project uses it at end.

```
def fix_sum_exact(h,n): ...
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize, minimize_scalar


def compute_c5(h):
    n = len(h)
    if n <= 0:
        return float('inf')
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)


def compute_I(h):
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    I = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    if n <= 0:
        return I
    one_minus = 1.0 - h
    I[off] = float(np.dot(h, one_minus))
    for d in range(1, n):
        I[off + d] = float(np.dot(h[d:], one_minus[:-d]))
        I[off - d] = float(np.dot(h[:-d], one_minus[d:]))
    return I


def fix_sum_exact(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) < 1e-15 or n <= 0:
        return h
    if diff > 0.0:
        order = np.argsort(1.0 - h)
        for j in order:
            room = 1.0 - float(h[j])
            if room <= 0.0:
                continue
            amt = min(room, diff)
            if amt > 0.0:
                h[j] += amt
                diff -= amt
            if abs(diff) < 1e-15:
                break
    else:
        order = np.argsort(h)
        for j in order:
            room = float(h[j])
            if room <= 0.0:
                continue
            amt = min(room, -diff)
            if amt > 0.0:
                h[j] -= amt
                diff += amt
            if abs(diff) < 1e-15:
                break
    return h


def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    if h.size != n:
        h = h[:n] if h.size > n else np.pad(h, (0, n - h.size), mode="edge")
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    for _ in range(120):
        diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(diff) < 1e-15:
            break
        mask = (h > 1e-14) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-14)
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
        if cnt > 0:
            h[mask] += diff / float(cnt)
        else:
            h += diff / float(n)
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return fix_sum_exact(h, n)


def normalize_sum_fast(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-14:
        return h
    mask = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt > 0:
        h[mask] += diff / float(cnt)
    else:
        h += diff / float(n)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def interp_to(src_h, src_n, dst_n):
    src_h = np.asarray(src_h, dtype=float)
    if src_n == dst_n:
        return src_h.copy()
    xp = (np.arange(src_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(src_n))
    xf = (np.arange(dst_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(dst_n))
    return np.interp(xf, xp, src_h)


def thue_morse_h(n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        v = i
        parity = 0
        while v:
            parity ^= (v & 1)
            v >>= 1
        h[i] = 1.0 if parity else 0.0
    return project_feasible(h, n)


def fourier_y(theta, t, modes):
    y = np.zeros_like(t, dtype=float)
    for a, m in zip(theta, modes):
        y = y + a * np.sin(float(m) * np.pi * t)
    q = np.tanh(y)
    mmean = float(np.mean(q))
    denom = 1.0 + abs(mmean)
    return (q - mmean) / denom


def fourier_obj(theta, t, modes, n):
    y = fourier_y(theta, t, modes)
    h = 0.5 * (1.0 + y)
    h = normalize_sum_fast(h, n)
    return compute_c5(h)


def generate_fourier_candidates(deadline, seed):
    out = []
    n = 224
    t = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
    modes = [1, 3, 5, 7, 9]
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed + 101)

    starts = []
    starts.append(np.array([0.9, 0.5, 0.25, 0.12, 0.05], dtype=float))
    starts.append(np.array([-0.9, -0.45, -0.22, -0.10, -0.04], dtype=float))
    for _ in range(3):
        starts.append(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0, size=len(modes)))

    for x0 in starts:
        if time.time() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        theta = x0.copy()
        try:
            res = minimize(
                fourier_obj,
                theta,
                method="Nelder-Mead",
                args=(t, modes, n),
                options={"maxiter": 70, "xatol": 1e-3, "fatol": 1e-7}
            )
            if hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None:
                theta = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        except Exception:
            theta = x0

        y = fourier_y(theta, t, modes)
        h = 0.5 * (1.0 + y)
        h = project_feasible(h, n)
        c = compute_c5(h)
        if np.isfinite(c):
            out.append((h, n, float(c)))
    return out


def pair_build_data(n, h, I, p, q, r_all):
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    x0 = float(h[p])
    y0 = float(h[q])
    S = x0 + y0
    low = max(0.0, S - 1.0)
    high = min(1.0, S)
    if high - low < 1e-13:
        return None

    mask = (r_all != p) & (r_all != q)
    if not np.any(mask):
        base = I.copy()
        const = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
        coeff = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    else:
        rm = r_all[mask]
        hm = h[rm]
        om = 1.0 - hm

        base = I.copy()
        const = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
        coeff = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)

        idx = p - rm + off
        base[idx] -= x0 * om
        coeff[idx] += om

        idx = q - rm + off
        base[idx] -= y0 * om
        const[idx] += S * om
        coeff[idx] -= om

        idx = rm - p + off
        base[idx] -= hm * (1.0 - x0)
        const[idx] += hm
        coeff[idx] -= hm

        idx = rm - q + off
        base[idx] -= hm * (1.0 - y0)
        const[idx] += hm * (1.0 - S)
        coeff[idx] += hm

    base[off] -= x0 * (1.0 - x0) + y0 * (1.0 - y0)
    idxp = off + (p - q)
    idxq = off + (q - p)
    base[idxp] -= x0 * (1.0 - y0)
    base[idxq] -= y0 * (1.0 - x0)

    bc = base + const
    work = np.empty_like(bc)
    return {
        "low": float(low),
        "high": float(high),
        "x0": float(x0),
        "S": float(S),
        "off": off,
        "idxp": idxp,
        "idxq": idxq,
        "bc": bc,
        "c": coeff,
        "work": work
    }


def pair_eval(x, data):
    x = float(x)
    work = data["work"]
    np.multiply(data["c"], x, out=work)
    work += data["bc"]
    S = data["S"]
    off = data["off"]
    work[off] += (S - S * S) + 2.0 * S * x - 2.0 * x * x
    work[data["idxp"]] += S * x - x * x
    work[data["idxq"]] += S - (S + 1.0) * x + x * x
    return float(np.max(work))


def pair_quick(data):
    low = data["low"]
    high = data["high"]
    length = high - low
    if length <= 1e-13:
        return float(pair_eval(data["x0"], data)), data["x0"]

    xs = np.linspace(low, high, 12)
    xs = np.append(xs, data["x0"])
    xs = np.clip(xs, low, high)
    xs = np.unique(xs)

    best_v = float("inf")
    best_x = data["x0"]
    for xx in xs:
        v = pair_eval(float(xx), data)
        if v < best_v:
            best_v = v
            best_x = float(xx)
    return best_v, best_x


def pair_refine(data, quick_x):
    low = data["low"]
    high = data["high"]
    best_x = float(np.clip(quick_x, low, high))
    best_v = pair_eval(best_x, data)

    length = high - low
    width = max(0.22 * length, 1e-8)
    lo = max(low, best_x - width)
    hi = min(high, best_x + width)
    if hi - lo > 1e-12:
        try:
            res = minimize_scalar(
                pair_eval,
                bounds=(lo, hi),
                args=(data,),
                method="bounded",
                options={"xatol": 1e-11, "maxiter": 45}
            )
            xx = float(np.clip(res.x, lo, hi))
            vv = pair_eval(xx, data)
            if vv < best_v:
                best_v = vv
                best_x = xx
        except Exception:
            pass

    if length > 1e-8:
        try:
            res = minimize_scalar(
                pair_eval,
                bounds=(low, high),
                args=(data,),
                method="bounded",
                options={"xatol": 1e-10, "maxiter": 30}
            )
            xx = float(np.clip(res.x, low, high))
            vv = pair_eval(xx, data)
            if vv < best_v:
                best_v = vv
                best_x = xx
        except Exception:
            pass

    return float(np.clip(best_x, low, high)), float(best_v)


def try_pair_update(h, I, n, p, q, r_all):
    if p == q:
        return False
    S = float(h[p]) + float(h[q])
    low = max(0.0, S - 1.0)
    high = min(1.0, S)
    if high - low < 1e-13:
        return False

    data = pair_build_data(n, h, I, p, q, r_all)
    if data is None:
        return False

    cur_max = float(np.max(I))
    qv, qx = pair_quick(data)
    if qv >= cur_max - 1e-11:
        return False

    xv, xv_v = pair_refine(data, qx)
    if xv_v >= cur_max - 1e-11:
        return False

    x_old = data["x0"]
    y_old = S - x_old
    x_new = float(xv)
    y_new = S - x_new
    if x_new < -1e-12 or x_new > 1.0 + 1e-12 or y_new < -1e-12 or y_new > 1.0 + 1e-12:
        return False
    x_new = float(np.clip(x_new, 0.0, 1.0))
    y_new = S - x_new
    y_new = float(np.clip(y_new, 0.0, 1.0))

    dv = pair_eval(x_new, data)
    if dv >= cur_max - 1e-11:
        return False

    off = n - 1
    delta = x_new - x_old
    mask = (r_all != p) & (r_all != q)
    if np.any(mask):
        rm = r_all[mask]
        hm = h[rm]
        om = 1.0 - hm
        I[p - rm + off] += delta * om
        I[q - rm + off] += -delta * om
        I[rm - p + off] += -delta * hm
        I[rm - q + off] += delta * hm

    old_self = x_old * (1.0 - x_old) + y_old * (1.0 - y_old)
    new_self = x_new * (1.0 - x_new) + y_new * (1.0 - y_new)
    I[off] += new_self - old_self

    idxp = off + (p - q)
    idxq = off + (q - p)
    I[idxp] += x_new * (1.0 - y_new) - x_old * (1.0 - y_old)
    I[idxq] += y_new * (1.0 - x_new) - y_old * (1.0 - x_old)

    h[p] = x_new
    h[q] = y_new
    return True


def make_candidates(h, n, I, rng):
    if n <= 1:
        return []
    if n <= 240:
        batch_max = 130
    elif n <= 420:
        batch_max = 100
    else:
        batch_max = 80

    L = 2 * n - 1
    off = n - 1
    pairs = set()

    top_k = min(6, L - 1)
    if top_k <= 0:
        top_idx = np.array([], dtype=int)
    else:
        top_idx = np.argpartition(I, -top_k)[-top_k:]
        top_idx = top_idx[np.argsort(I[top_idx])[::-1]]

    for li in top_idx:
        idx = int(li)
        d = idx - off
        if d > 0:
            a = np.arange(d, n)
            b = np.arange(n - d)
            terms = h[a] * (1.0 - h[b])
            k = min(6, len(a))
            if k <= 0:
                continue
            tt = np.argpartition(terms, -k)[-k:]
            for t0 in tt:
                u = int(a[t0])
                v = int(b[t0])
                if u == v:
                    continue
                if u > v:
                    u, v = v, u
                pairs.add(u * n + v)
        elif d < 0:
            m = -d
            a = np.arange(n - m)
            b = a + m
            terms = h[a] * (1.0 - h[b])
            k = min(6, len(a))
            if k <= 0:
                continue
            tt = np.argpartition(terms, -k)[-k:]
            for t0 in tt:
                u = int(a[t0])
                v = int(b[t0])
                if u == v:
                    continue
                if u > v:
                    u, v = v, u
                pairs.add(u * n + v)
        else:
            terms = h * (1.0 - h)
            k = min(6, n)
            if k <= 0:
                continue
            tt = np.argpartition(terms, -k)[-k:]
            tt = tt[:k]
            for i0 in range(k):
                u = int(tt[i0])
                for i1 in range(i0 + 1, k):
                    v = int(tt[i1])
                    if u == v:
                        continue
                    if u > v:
                        u, v = v, u
                    pairs.add(u * n + v)

    if n >= 6:
        hi_cnt = min(3, n // 2)
        lo_cnt = min(3, n // 2)
        hi = np.argpartition(h, -hi_cnt)[-hi_cnt:]
        lo = np.argpartition(h, lo_cnt)[:lo_cnt]
        for u in hi:
            u = int(u)
            for v in lo:
                v = int(v)
                if u == v:
                    continue
                if u > v:
                    u, v = v, u
                pairs.add(u * n + v)

    while len(pairs) < batch_max:
        u = int(rng.randint(0, n))
        v = int(rng.randint(0, n))
        if u == v:
            continue
        if u > v:
            u, v = v, u
        pairs.add(u * n + v)

    return list(pairs)


def perturb_h(h, n, rng, sigma):
    hp = h.copy()
    m = min(n, max(3, n // 25))
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
    hp[idx] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=m)
    return project_feasible(hp, n)


def optimize_pairs(h, n, deadline, seed):
    if n <= 1:
        c = compute_c5(h)
        return project_feasible(h, n if n > 0 else 1), c, max(1, n)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed + 777)
    r_all = np.arange(n, dtype=int)

    h = project_feasible(h, n).copy()
    I = compute_I(h)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_max = float(np.max(I))
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)

    accept_count = 0
    no_improve = 0

    while time.time() < deadline - 1.5:
        if accept_count % 100 == 0:
            I = compute_I(h)
            mcur = float(np.max(I))
            if mcur < best_max:
                best_max = mcur
                best_h = h.copy()

        cur_max = float(np.max(I))
        if cur_max < best_max:
            best_max = cur_max
            best_h = h.copy()

        cands = make_candidates(h, n, I, rng)
        improved_batch = False

        for code in cands:
            if time.time() > deadline - 0.6:
                break
            p = int(code // n)
            q = int(code % n)
            before = float(np.max(I))
            if try_pair_update(h, I, n, p, q, r_all):
                after = float(np.max(I))
                if after < before - 1e-13:
                    improved_batch = True
                    accept_count += 1
                    if after < best_max:
                        best_max = after
                        best_h = h.copy()
                elif after < best_max:
                    best_max = after
                    best_h = h.copy()

        if improved_batch:
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve <= 18:
                sigma = 0.02 + 0.004 * float(no_improve)
                hp = perturb_h(h, n, rng, sigma)
                Ip = compute_I(hp)
                mp = float(np.max(Ip))
                curp = float(np.max(I))
                esc = max(1.0, 0.025 * max(1.0, best_max))
                if mp < best_max + esc and mp < curp + esc:
                    h = hp
                    I = Ip
                if mp < best_max:
                    best_max = mp
                    best_h = hp.copy()
            elif no_improve > 24:
                break

    h1 = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    c1 = compute_c5(h1)
    h2 = project_feasible(h, n)
    c2 = compute_c5(h2)
    if c2 < c1:
        return h2, float(c2), n
    return h1, float(c1), n


def add_candidate(candidates, h, n):
    if h is None or n <= 0:
        return
    h = project_feasible(h, int(n))
    c = compute_c5(h)
    if np.isfinite(c):
        candidates.append((h, int(n), float(c)))


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(5.0, float(budget_s))
    end_global = t0 + total - 20.0
    if end_global < t0 + 5.0:
        end_global = t0 + max(2.0, total - 2.0)

    all_cands = []
    parent_h = None

    try:
        ph = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if ph.size > 1:
            ph = project_feasible(ph, ph.size)
            pn = int(ph.size)
            parent_h = ph
            add_candidate(all_cands, ph, pn)

            sym = 0.5 * (ph + ph[::-1])
            sym = project_feasible(sym, pn)
            add_candidate(all_cands, sym, pn)

            target_ns = [256, 320, 480, 512]
            twice = 2 * pn
            if twice not in target_ns and 2 <= twice <= 768:
                target_ns.append(twice)

            for tn in target_ns:
                tn = int(tn)
                if tn <= 0 or tn == pn or tn > 800:
                    continue
                hi = interp_to(ph, pn, tn)
                add_candidate(all_cands, hi, tn)
                if tn % pn == 0:
                    hd = np.repeat(ph, tn // pn)
                    add_candidate(all_cands, hd, tn)

            for tn in [256, 320]:
                tn = int(tn)
                if tn == pn or tn > 800:
                    continue
                hs = interp_to(sym, pn, tn)
                add_candidate(all_cands, hs, tn)
    except Exception:
        parent_h = None

    if parent_h is None:
        add_candidate(all_cands, thue_morse_h(256), 256)

    if end_global - time.time() > 28.0:
        four_dead = min(end_global, time.time() + 45.0)
        four = generate_fourier_candidates(four_dead, seed)
        if four:
            cvals = np.empty(len(four), dtype=float)
            for i, item in enumerate(four):
                cvals[i] = item[2]
            best_four = four[int(np.argmin(cvals))]
            add_candidate(all_cands, best_four[0], best_four[1])
            nf = best_four[1]
            if nf != 320:
                hf = interp_to(best_four[0], nf, 320)
                add_candidate(all_cands, hf, 320)

    if not all_cands:
        add_candidate(all_cands, thue_morse_h(256), 256)

    selected = []
    if all_cands:
        cvals = np.empty(len(all_cands), dtype=float)
        for i, item in enumerate(all_cands):
            cvals[i] = item[2]
        order = np.argsort(cvals)
        for oi in order:
            selected.append(all_cands[int(oi)])
            if len(selected) == 3:
                break

    if selected:
        best_h, best_n, best_c = selected[0]
    else:
        h0 = thue_morse_h(256)
        best_h = h0
        best_n = 256
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h)

    if end_global - time.time() > 30.0:
        if len(selected) == 1:
            fr = [1.0]
        elif len(selected) == 2:
            fr = [0.65, 0.35]
        else:
            fr = [0.60, 0.26, 0.14]

        for i in range(len(selected)):
            if time.time() > end_global - 6.0:
                break
            rem = max(0.0, end_global - time.time())
            if rem <= 0.0:
                break
            alloc = rem * fr[i]
            if rem > 20.0:
                alloc = max(15.0, min(alloc, rem - 5.0))
            else:
                alloc = rem
            dl = min(end_global, time.time() + alloc)
            h2, c2, n2 = optimize_pairs(selected[i][0], selected[i][1], dl, seed + 917 + i)
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_h = project_feasible(h2, n2)
                best_n = int(n2)
                best_c = compute_c5(best_h)
            rem = max(0.0, end_global - time.time())

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    final_c = compute_c5(best_h)
    return best_h, float(final_c), int(best_n)
```
